Maybe optimal h is a "low-discrepancy" function: h(x)=1 if fractional part of x in some set with carefully chosen lengths to avoid k. ### Better local solver ideas

Need choose α (temperature) carefully. LSE objective = (1/α) log ∑ exp(α c_j - α max) + max? Actually c values not centered? Implement stable:
Let c = directed terms * dx (length 2n-1). m = max(c). sm = α*(c-m). If max(sm)>500, use m + (max(sm)+log(sum(exp(sm-maxsm))))/α else m + logsumexp(sm)/α. This equals m + logsumexp(α(c-m))/α; when α large and c close, approximates m + small. At α→∞, smooth max→m. Gradient: weights w_j = exp(α(c_j-m))/sum. Then ∇Φ=Σ w_j ∇c_j. Since adding constant m? derivative of logsumexp α(c-m) wrt c_j = α w_j, divided by α = w_j; plus derivative of m? We defined Φ=m+LSE(α(c-m))/α. Derivative wrt c_j = δ_{j*=argmax}? + w_j - (Σ w_k ∂m/∂c_k) = δ + w - w (since m derivative one-hot same weighted by w? Actually ∂m=one, sum w one =1, cancels) => w_j. So gradient =Σ w_j ∇c_j. Good. Sum constraint: SLSQP equality.

But SLSQP may struggle with large α because gradient weights concentrate; fine. Use alphas increasing: 20,100,500,2000,10000,50000,200000,1e6. But at too high alpha, LSE gradient discontinuous; okay for SLSQP? It can handle. Alternatively use "log barrier" active set.

Could also minimize `t + β Φ(α)`? No, direct smooth.

Important: `c` terms are already scaled by dx. The gradient terms ∇c_k must include dx factor. We'll compute weights w and then gradient of each directed term times dx. `grad_term(k)` should multiply by dx when summing? If we store c=term*dx, ∇c = dx * ∇term. We can incorporate dx in grad loop.

SLSQP callable:

```
def slsqp_obj(x,n,alpha):
    c=compute_c_vec(x,n)
    m=c.max()
    s=alpha*(c-m)
    mx=s.max()
    if mx>500:
        return m + (mx+np.log(np.sum(np.exp(s-mx))))/alpha
    else:
        return m + np.log(np.sum(np.exp(s)))/alpha  # since m? if not subtract mx?
```
Careful: if subtract mx, logsumexp(α(c-m)) = mx + logsumexp(s-mx). So objective = m + (mx + LSE(s-mx))/alpha. Good.
If no mx (values large negative) use m + LSE? Could be underflow; stable.

Gradient:
```
w = exp(α(c-m)-LSE) # same weights
g = grad_weighted(x,n,w)
return g
```

`grad_weighted`:
We need gradient of smooth objective with weights w on each directed term (order known). Could loop over k=0..n-1 with weights wp, wn.
For k=0: pk0 and nk0 both same; gradient of term: ∑(1-2h_i) for pk, same for nk. If total weight w0 = wp0+wn0, add w0*dx*(1-2h).
For k>0:
pk weight wp: (grad[0:nv] += (1-h[k:])); (grad[k:] += -h[:nv])
nk weight wn: (grad[0:nv] += -h[k:]); (grad[k:] += (1-h[:nv]))
Combined:
grad[0:nv] += dx*( wp*(1-h[k:]) + wn*(-h[k:]) ) = dx*((wp-wn)*(1-h)?? Actually wp*(1-h) - wn*h.
grad[k:n] += dx*( -wp*h[:nv] + wn*(1-h[:nv]) ) = dx*( wn*(1-h) - wp*h).
Combine vectorized:
```
a = wp - wn
b = wn + wp
# left: wp*(1-H) - wn*H = wp - (wp+wn)H = b? Letleft = wp - (wp+wn)H.
left = wp - s*Hleft
right = wn - s*Hright
```
Where Hleft=h[k:n], Hright=h[0:nv]. If wp=wn, left=wp(1-2H), as expected negative of? For symmetric weights.
We'll loop k and add. Use local variables for speed.

But SLSQP gradient calls compute c each time, then weights, then grad. We can combine: objective returns value; gradient separate. That recomputes c. SciPy may call objective and gradient separately, doubling. We can use a class to cache last x/c? But no closures? Class with methods allowed? "helper functions top level, no closures or lambdas". We can define a class? Top-level functions can use global cache? Need no lambdas. We can implement global variables? Better: define functions that take `cache` dict? But minimize requires callables with x. ### Pair / coordinate refinement

After smooth optimization, do local discrete moves to reduce exact max (not smoothed). We can implement **best-improvement pair moves with fast c update**. Full recompute correlate each move O(n log n) in C okay for thousands. But for many pairs too slow. We can use local active lags: Instead of all 2n-1, track current c vector. For a proposed move, only lags where h changes? Actually all lags affected because term includes h_i and h_j with shifts; but we can update c vector incrementally in O(n) per pair? For move i->j, for every lag k, pk_k changes if i involved or j involved. There are O(n) terms (two positions per lag). We can update full c vector in Python O(n) using vectorized shifts, then max. That could be faster than full correlate? Maybe.

But to improve last bits, simple local search over random pairs with line search may suffice.

Let's design a **local coordinate pair optimizer**:
- Maintain h, n, current c vector (unnormalized terms times dx) and max.
- For a set of candidate moves (i,j,δ or direction), update c efficiently?
Simpler: For each i, choose j based on gradient of exact max? Use active lag weights: compute weights w from top lags (hard assignment maybe top M equally). Compute directional derivative d = ∇F_i approx w·∇c. Move from high derivative? Actually to reduce max, we want direction p with p·g<0. Project to sum zero. Steepest p_i = -(g_i - mean). Then propose moving mass from largest positive? We can do:
   - Compute active weights from top K lags (not smooth max) to get gradient g.
   - Feasible direction d = -(g - mean) maybe.
   - Use top/bottom coordinates: move from i where d_i>0 large to j where d_i<0 large, step approximated.
This is like pair search gradient but better.

But previous gradient_pair_search fixed step small and all pairs O(n^2), too slow. We can optimize top ranks only (e.g. top/bottom 30 gradient components) and line search.

Let's use computational strategy rather than analytic.

### Low-dimensional ansatz combinations

We can afford to do a numeric local optimization over the full n=300 from many starting points, including radial/trapezoid/smoothstep/periodic functions. Then the local optimizer can refine. If one of these starts in a better basin, final improves.

We need ensure within budget. Main cost local optimizer. We can run local optimizer on, say, 5 candidate seeds for 100s each? Need best.

Maybe the previous record is already from the global optimum of the discrete problem; to improve, need better numerical optimizer or different n. Starting point variations may not help if local same. But if previous got stuck, yes.

Let's identify likely better starting functions:
1. **Radial smoothstep**: g(r)=1-f(r) where f smoothstep r^2(3-2r). This gives central plateau, ends zero. Need compute discrete h at n: r=|i - (n-1)/2|/( (n-1)/2 )? Domain [0,2]. Actually if samples represent midpoints? Use x=2*(i+0.5)/n, r=abs(x-1). Good. g=1 - 3r^2 +2r^3. Area not exactly 1 due discrete; project_feasible.
2. **Trapezoid plateau a**: h=1 if r≤a else max(0, 1 - (r-a)/(1-a)). Try a values 0.2,0.3,0.4,0.5.
3. **Cosine radial**: h = 0.5 + 0.5 * cos(π * r^p?)? Need average .5. Let h=0.5+0.5*cos(π r^p) with r center0 peak1, end0 if p>0? ∫ not .5; project can adjust? Projection to area 1 may alter. Use h=cos^2(π r^p/2)? Maybe.
4. **Fourier/Laplacian regularized random seeds**: Generate h as 0.5 + sum small cos modes, project.
5. **Initial_h_values** itself and its interpolation/projection.
6. **Mirror/centering** not change radial? Mirror same.

Then run local optimizer on best few. We can also perform **coarse-to-fine optimization**: Optimize at n=128 or 160 from seeds, then upsample to n=300 and refine. Coarse allows faster global exploration and can find better basin. But previous tried 150,200,300. We can use coarse random many seeds.

### Optimizer implementation details

Let's plan code compact but robust.

Top-level functions:
- `compute_c_vec(h,n)`: returns c directed terms * dx. Use autocorr `np.correlate(h,h,'full')` and prefix sums. Need ensure length 2n-1 order not matter for max but for weights gradient aligned. Let's define array indices:
  idx = n-1 central k=0 pk. For positive k=0..n-1 at n-1+k: value = (prefix[n-k] - A_k)*dx.
  For negative k at n-1-k (k=1..n-1): value = (total - prefix[n-k] - A_k)*dx.
  This matches full correlate? It includes both directed terms. `np.max(c_vec)` equals `np.max(np.correlate(h,1-h)*dx)`? Let's verify. Full correlate of h and 1-h: For lag k positive? According to docs, c[m] = Σ_i a_{i+m} v_i? For a=h,v=1-h. Positive m≥0: Σ_i h[i+m]*(1-h[i]) = nk_m (our negative term for shift +m? We call nk because h shifted as first? Actually our nk=Σ_{i=0}^{n-m-1} h_i+m (1-h_i). Yes exactly c_positive_lag = nk_m. Negative m: pk_m. So our pk/nk are included. The max order doesn't matter. Good.
  Need handle floating: `A=np.correlate(h,h,'full'); A_k=A[n-1+k]` for k≥0. `prefix=np.concatenate(([0],np.cumsum(h)))`; total=prefix[n]. For k=0: pk=total - A[n-1]; nk same. For k>0: pk=prefix[n-k] - A[n-1+k]; nk=total - prefix[n-k] - A[k? no same A]. Multiply dx.
  We should maybe use `A = np.correlate(h, h, mode='full')` but floating exact. Good.

- `smooth_value(h,n,alpha)` maybe uses c vector. For performance, we can compute c once in objective and cache. But `compute_c_vec` uses correlate; okay.

- `grad_c_weights(h,n,w)` returns gradient of c? Here w is weights over c vector (sum=1) for smooth objective. Need sum weight? weights compute as normalized. Then gradient of smooth objective = dx*Σ over terms weight * derivative term. Since c already includes dx. Let's incorporate dx in `compute_c_vec`, but `grad_c_weights` should multiply dx. Or store weights for unnormalized terms? Let's define compute_c_vec returns scaled by dx. Weights w length same. Gradient = dx * grad_unscaled terms. So multiply dx in grad loop.

Efficient grad loop with weights:
```
def grad_weighted(h,n,w):
    dx=2.0/n
    g=np.zeros(n)
    # central k=0 index n-1, weight w[n-1] represents both pk and nk? Wait our c vector has only one central term? But max of full correlate has one k=0. In directed terms, there are not two distinct pk and nk for k=0; we set c[n-1]=pk0 (same). For gradient, weight just central weight. derivative of pk0: ∂Σ h_i(1-h_i)=1-2h_i. So g += dx*w0*(1-2h).
    for k in 1..n-1:
       wp=w[n-1+k] (positive lag? Let's call term a[l]=nk?); wn=w[n-1-k]. Need align with our definitions.
```
We need know which weight corresponds to pk/nk. In compute_c_vec:
- for k>0, at index n-1+k: set pk_k or nk_k? Let's choose index positive = pk_k? But actual correlate full positive maybe nk. For max, no matter; but gradient must match assigned term. So define c[n-1+k] = pk_k = prefix[n-k] - A_k. c[n-1-k] = nk_k = total - prefix[n-k] - A_k. Then gradient as derived for pk at positive index. Good.
Then:
```
wp = w[n-1+k]; wn = w[n-1-k]
nv=n-k
Hleft = h[k:n]  # h[i+k] for i=0..nv-1
Hright = h[0:nv] # h[i-k] for i=k..n-1
# pk contribution: if weight wp: g[0:nv] += (1-Hleft); g[k:n] += -Hright
# nk contribution: if weight wn: g[0:nv] += -Hleft; g[k:n] += (1-Hright)
left = wp*(1-Hleft) - wn*Hleft = wp - (wp+wn)*Hleft
right = -wp*Hright + wn*(1-Hright) = wn - (wp+wn)*Hright
g[0:nv] += left
g[k:n] += right
```
Multiply dx at end or per: return dx*g.
For k=0 central weight w0: g += w0*(1-2h), then *dx.
This loops k=1..n-1. Use local variables for speed. Could vectorize over k using convolution? Not necessary.

Potential optimization: If wp or wn zero, skip. Exp weights may underflow; many zeros. High α weights concentrated. For low α, many weights but LSE grad smooth.

- `lse_stats(h,n,alpha)`: compute c, value, weights. But to avoid c/weights recompute in objective/grad, use cache with key? We'll implement:
```
def _object(x, n, alpha, cache):
   c = compute_c_vec(x,n)
   m = c.max()
   s = alpha*(c-m)
   s -= s.max()
   z = np.sum(np.exp(s))
   val = m + (s.max()? already 0? Actually smax) ...
```
Wait if we subtract max `sm = alpha*(c-m); mx=sm.max(); s=sm-mx; lse=mx+log(sum(exp(s)))`; val=m+lse/alpha. Weights = exp(s)/sum. If mx large? Since mx=0 after subtract? Actually if s = sm - mx, max=0; `sum_exp = np.sum(np.exp(s))`; val = m + (mx + log(sum_exp))/alpha = m + (0? no mx original) + log(sum_exp) over alpha.
```
 cache['c']=c; cache['w']=np.exp(s)/sum_exp; cache['m']=m
 return val
```

def radial_values(n, a=0.0, smooth=False):
    x = (np.arange(n)+0.5)*2.0/n  # midpoints
    r = np.abs(x-1.0)
    if smooth:
        f = r*r*(3-2*r)
        h = 1 - f  # mean? ∫ r^2(3-2r)=? Smoothstep f has mean 0.5? ∫0^1 3r^2-2r^3=1-0.5=0.5. Yes ∫f=0.5. Thus ∫h=0.5, area=1. Good discrete projection.
    elif a:
        # trapezoid plateau a: h=1 if r<=a else max(0,1-(r-a)/(1-a)) for r<=1, else 0. But for a=0, triangle 1-r.
        h = np.where(r <= a, 1.0, np.maximum(0.0, 1.0 - (r-a)/(1.0-a)))
        # at r=1, 0. area? plateau length area? discrete ok.
    else:
        h=1-r
    return project_feasible(h,n)
```

```

def spectral_random(n, M, rng, amplitude=1.0):
    vals=np.full(n,0.5)
    idx=np.arange(n)
    for m in range(1,M+1):
       a=rng.normal(0,amplitude/m)
       b=rng.normal(0,amplitude/m)
       vals += (a*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*idx/n)+b*np.sin(...))
    return project_feasible(vals,n)

### Detailed run design

Let's write pseudo:
```
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize, differential_evolution
import time

# helpers...

def run(seed=42,budget_s=1100,**kwargs):
    t0=time.time(); deadline=t0+budget_s-4
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    # parent
    try:
       n0=len(initial_h_values); h0=np.array(initial_h_values,float).copy()
    except:
       n0=300; h0=make_radial(n0,'smooth')
    if n0 not in (256,300): maybe keep.
    best_h=project_feasible(h0,n0); best_c=compute_c5(best_h,n0); best_n=n0
    # list candidates (n, h)
    cands=[]
    def consider(h,n):
       h=project_feasible(h,n); c=compute_c5(h,n); cands.append((c,h,n))
    consider(h0,n0)
    for kind/a:
       consider(make_radial(n0,'plateau',a),n0)
       ...
    # maybe coarse parent resample to 256,384?
    for n_alt in [256,384]: consider(resample(h0,n0,n_alt), n_alt)
    # sort by c, take top max 5 candidates. But seed h0 likely best. Also keep radial top.
    cands.sort(key=lambda t:t[0])
    selected=[]
    seen_n? We can select up to 6 distinct.
    # Phase 1: quick optimize each selected with time slice
    for c,h,n in selected[:6]:
       rem=deadline-time.time()
       if rem<80: break
       t_end=min(time.time()+70, deadline-300)
       h,c=local_refine(h,n,t_end, quick=True)
       if c<best_c: update best
    # Phase 2: Strong optimize best (and maybe second) with remaining
    t_end=min(time.time()+300, deadline-150)
    best_h,best_c=local_refine(best_h,best_n,t_end, quick=False)
    # Phase 3: gradient pair refine and final pair search
    if time<deadline-20: gradient_pair_refine...
    if time<deadline-5: pair_search small...
    # Final: maybe try slightly larger n by interpolating best? If time >60: n_try= len*1.25? optimize quick.
    best_h=project_feasible; best_c=compute_c5
    return best_h,best_c,len
```

SLSQP function:
```
def slsqp_alpha(h,n,alpha,maxiter):
    state={}
    bs=[(0,1)]*n
    cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':_sum_fun,'jac':_sum_jac,'args':(n,)}]
    res=minimize(_lse_obj, h, jac=_lse_grad, args=(n,alpha,state), method='SLSQP', bounds=bs, constraints=cons, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-14,'disp':False})
    return project_feasible(res.x,n)
```
But calling with same `bounds` list each time okay. Need note SLSQP may modify bounds? no.

Implement `_lse_obj(x,n,alpha,state)`:
```
c=compute_c_vec(x,n)
m=c.max()
s=alpha*(c-m)
mx=s.max()
s=s-mx
se=np.exp(s)
z=se.sum()
value = m + (mx + np.log(z))/alpha
state['c']=c; state['w']=se/z; state['x']=x
return value
```
If alpha very large, alpha*(c-m) maybe for c-m negative large; underflow okay. mx maybe 0 (for max) always 0 because one c-m=0 => s max=0. Actually c-m has max 0, so mx=0 always! No need mx. s=alpha*(c-m), max 0. se max 1. z≥1. val=m + log(z)/alpha. Good simpler. For c-m negative large, s underflows to 0, no overflow. Great. If m NaN? no.
Gradient weights = exp(alpha*(c-m))/z. Sum 1. For high alpha, only max c ~1.
```
def _lse_grad(x,n,alpha,state):
    if state.get('w') is None or state['x'] is None or state['x'].shape != x.shape or not np.array_equal(state['x'], x):
         # recompute
         c=compute_c_vec(x,n); m=c.max(); s=alpha*(c-m); z=np.exp(s).sum(); w=np.exp(s)/z
    else: w=state['w']
    return grad_weighted(x,n,w)
```
But if SciPy calls grad with x that is exactly same object? `np.array_equal` okay. If x has tiny differences due SLSQP? It should call with same as obj in a pair; but if not, recompute. `state['x']` reference; if SLSQP mutates array in place? It may pass a copy; if it mutates original? We store as `x.copy()` to be safe? `state['x']=x.copy()` in obj. This O(n). Then array_equal. In grad, recompute if not equal. Good.
But if objective called, then gradient called with same x but `state['x']` copy equals x, use. If SLSQP changes x after obj? It shouldn't until next iteration. Good.

Potential issue: The LSE objective value `m + log(z)/alpha` tends to max; but if multiple lags equally max, z>1. Gradient weights spread. Good.

`compute_c_vec` uses `np.correlate(h,h,'full')`; if h contains tiny outside [0,1] from SLSQP, products maybe >? Should be okay. Maybe clip in compute? For objective, if h slightly outside bounds, c might be lower? SLSQP bounds enforce.

`grad_weighted`: Need ensure weights sum maybe not exactly 1; okay. Could use `if wp+wn ==0` skip. For speed, precompute `s=Wsum` and use operations.

```
def grad_weighted(h,n,w):
    dx=2.0/n
    g=np.zeros(n)
    w0=w[n-1]
    if w0 !=0: g += w0*(1-2*h)
    for k in range(1,n):
       wp=w[n-1+k]; wn=w[n-1-k]
       ws=wp+wn
       if ws ==0: continue
       nv=n-k
       Hl=h[k:nv+k]? h[k:]
       Hr=h[:nv]
       g[:nv] += wp - ws*Hl
       g[k:] += wn - ws*Hr
    return dx*g
```
Wait left formula `wp - ws*Hl`; if ws=0 skip. But if wp=0, left=-wn*Hl, correct. If wn=0, left=wp*(1-Hl), correct. Right `wn - ws*Hr`, correct.
Outer slices with `nv` variable. `Hl=h[k:]` and `Hr=h[:n-k]`. Good.
For k near n, slices length 1.

Could optimize by using `g[:nv] = g[:nv] + (wp - ws*h[k:])` creates temp. Fine.

### Pair refine exact active weights

Implement `gradient_pair_refine` with top active weights:
```
def gradient_pair_refine(h,n,deadline,p=14,passes=2000):
    best=compute_c5(h,n)
    h=project_feasible
    for _ in range(passes):
       if time.time() > deadline: break
       c=compute_c_vec(h,n); m=c.max()
       # top 24 active
       idx=np.argsort(c)[-24:][::-1]
       w=np.zeros(2*n-1)
       # weights softmax with temperature maybe 0.005; use (c-m)/0.005
       tmp=(c[idx]-m)/0.005
       tmp=tmp-tmp.max(); ww=np.exp(tmp)/sum
       w[idx]=ww
       g=grad_weighted(h,n,w)
       g-=g.mean()
       sources=np.argsort(g)[-p:][::-1]
       targets=np.argsort(g)[:p]
       improved=False
       for s in sources:
          for t in targets:
             if s==t: continue
             cap=min(h[s],1-h[t])
             if cap<=1e-12: continue
             for frac in (0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01):
                d=cap*frac
                if d<=1e-14: continue
                h[s]-=d; h[t]+=d
                c2=compute_c5(h,n)
                if c2 < best - 1e-14:
                   best=c2; improved=True
                   break
                h[s]+=d; h[t]-=d
             if improved:
                # For speed, continue or break? We'll continue to try more, but h changed and g stale; after accept project? 
                h=project_feasible(h,n)
        if not improved:
              # maybe try smaller p/temp? break after a few nonimproved
    return h,best
```
This can be slow if after one accept continues many pairs with stale g. But each pair fractions compute; maybe okay. Could after accept break out of whole pass to recompute g, but then number of evals per improvement high. Let's compromise: after accept, set `break_pair_loops` with nested flag to restart pass. This makes steepest but more recomputes. We'll implement with for-else or flags. If no improvement, decrease temperature/p after a few? We can have multiple alpha in loop? Simpler: If no improvement, break (local min for top active). Then call again with active temp smaller? Not.

Maybe better: Use fixed small moves all candidates to polish. Previous pair_search with radius works for fine polishing. We'll include a final `pair_search_small(h,n,deadline,deltas,radius=30)`.

`pair_search_small` optimized: It moves δ between pairs within radius or top gradient. We can implement with random pairs? Let's use gradient top to generate pairs.

```
def polish_pairs(h,n,deadline,deltas):
   best=compute_c5(h,n)
   for d in deltas:
      # use gradient active to order pairs each pass
      c=compute_c_vec; weights; g; order sources/targets
      while time<deadline:
         improved=False
         for s in sources: for t in targets: if within? move d if feasible, eval accept
         if not improved: break
   return
```

### Exact water-filling projection

Let's implement robust final projection and fast for DE.

For final, `project_feasible` with up to 200 iterations, then exact correction. To avoid slow loops if many saturated: Proportional update may stall if diff small but cannot due floating. We can use binary search of threshold for exact projection. Let's implement a **fast exact projection** using bisection on λ for all uses? It may be more reliable and vectorized.
Projection onto {0≤y≤1, sum=target}:
Find λ such that sum(clip(h - λ,0,1))=target.
Function monotone decreasing in λ. λ can be from min(h)-1 to max(h)+1. Bisection 60 iterations:
```
def project_exact(h,n):
    h=np.asarray(h,float)
    target=n/2
    lo=-1.0; hi=1.0+max(max(h)-0.5,0)?
```
Let's define a range: If λ very negative, y=1 all sum=n. If λ very positive, y=0 sum=0. We need target. We can binary search λ in [min(h)-1, max(h)+1] maybe. For each, y=np.clip(h-lam,0,1); s=y.sum(); if s>target, need increase λ (decrease y); else decrease λ. 50 iterations O(n). For n=300, 15k ops, in C with np.clip, maybe faster than proportional Python loops? 50 vectorized clips 300 =>15k, fine. For many DE evals at n=96, 50*96=4800 C ops, okay. Let's use exact bisection for all. Need handle if due to bounds target always in [0,n]. Good.
Bisection bounds: Let a=np.min(h)-1.0, b=np.max(h)+1.0. At λ=a, h-lam ≥ h-(min-1)≥1 for all? If h_i=min, diff=1; others >1, clip=1 => sum n. At λ=b, h-lam ≤ -1 for all, clip=0. So sum at low n, high 0. We need target. We want y sum target. If lambda increases, sum decreases. Binary:
```
lo=a  # sum ~ n (too high if target<n)
hi=b # sum ~0 (too low)
for 50:
  mid=(lo+hi)/2
  s=np.clip(h-mid,0,1).sum()
  if s>target: lo=mid
  else: hi=mid
y=np.clip(h-(lo+hi)/2,0,1)
# adjust residual exactly on non-saturated:
res=target-y.sum()
# find idx where 0<y<1, add res spread? To get exact, distribute to largest room? Simple choose indices not at bounds.
if abs(res)>1e-15:
   mask=(y>1e-12)&(y<1-1e-12)
   if not any: choose where res>0 y<1 else y>0
   y[mask] += res/ mask.sum()
   y=np.clip(y,0,1)
```

def local_refine(h,n,deadline,max_alphas,quick=False):
   h=project_exact(h.copy(),n); best=compute_c5(h,n)
   if quick: alphas=[20,100,500,2000]
   else: alphas=[20,100,500,2000,10000,50000,100000]
   maxiter_base = 300 if n<=200 else 150 if n<=400 else 80
   if quick: maxiter_base//=2
   for a in alphas:
      if time.time()>deadline: break
      mi=max(30, min(maxiter_base, 200 if quick else 300))

import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time

def compute_c5(h,n):
    dx=2.0/n
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full'))*dx)
```

`compute_c_vec`:
```
def compute_c_vec(h,n):
    dx=2.0/n
    A=np.correlate(h,h,mode='full')
    pref=np.empty(n+1); pref[0]=0.0; pref[1:]=np.cumsum(h)
    total=pref[n]
    c=np.empty(2*n-1)
    c[n-1]=(total-A[n-1])*dx
    for k in range(1,n):
        a=A[n-1+k]
        p=pref[n-k]
        pk=((p-a)*dx)
        nk=((total-p-a)*dx)
        c[n-1+k]=pk
        c[n-1-k]=nk
    return c
```
Loop n-1 Python; for each k just scalar arrays. For n=300 okay. Could vectorize using A segment and pref slice:
```

p=pref[0:n-1]? For k, n-k goes n-1 down to1. `p = pref[n-ks]` vector.
pk=(p-a)*dx; nk=(total-p-a)*dx
c[n:n]=pk; c[:n-1]=nk[::-1]? For k=1..n-1, c[n-1-k] descending: k=1 index n-2, k=n-1 index0. So `c[:n-1] = nk[::-1]` if nk order k=1..n-1, nk[k-1]. Let's check: nk[0]=k=1 -> index n-2. ```

target=pref[n - ks]

```
def compute_c_vec(h,n):
   dx=2.0/n
   A=np.correlate(h,h,'full')
   pref=np.concatenate(([0.0],np.cumsum(h)))
   total=pref[n]
   c=np.empty(2*n-1)
   c[n-1]=(total-A[n-1])*dx
   if n>1:
      ks=np.arange(1,n)
      Apos=A[n:n+ks[-1]] # A[n:2n-1]
      p=pref[n-ks]
      pk=(p-Apos)*dx
      nk=(total-p-Apos)*dx
      c[n:]=pk
      c[:n-1]=nk[::-1]
   return c
```
Check A index: n=2, ks=[1], A[2:3]? full length3 indices 0,1,2. central A[1]. Okay `A[2:3]`. Good. For n=300, `A[300:599]`. Good.

`grad_weighted` loop or vectorized? We can vectorize with cumulative? Maybe loop fine. Could optimize by precomputing? Let's keep loop.

```
def grad_weighted(h,n,w):
    g=np.zeros(n)
    w0=w[n-1]
    if w0:
       g += w0*(1.0-2.0*h)
    for k in range(1,n):
       wp=w[n-1+k]; wn=w[n-1-k]
       ws=wp+wn
       if ws == 0.0: continue
       nv=n-k
       g[:nv] += wp - ws*h[k:]
       g[k:] += wn - ws*h[:nv]
    return (2.0/n)*g  # g scaled? Wait the formulas derived for unscaled terms? Need multiply dx. We can either multiply at end by dx. But note ws weights sum 1, formula left = wp*(1-Hl)-wn*Hl unscaled derivative of terms. Multiplying dx at end okay.
```
But if we multiply at end, `g[:nv] += wp - ws*h[k:]`, etc. At k=0: g += w0*(1-2h). Then return dx*g. Good.
However if ws is very small but nonzero, okay. `if ws ==0` exact; weights from exp underflow might be zero. If wp/wn negative? no.

Let's test gradient for C0 only weight w0=1, h=0.5: g_i=1-1=0. For h=0.6, g=-0.2; returning dx*g. Direction to minimize LSE? gradient of c0 =1-2h. For h>0.5, gradient negative; moving h negative? minimize c0 -> increase h, yes.

`lse_parts`:
```
def lse_parts(x,n,alpha):
   c=compute_c_vec(x,n)
   m=float(np.max(c))
   s=alpha*(c-m)
   # ensure no nan: s = np.where(np.isfinite(s),s,-1000)
   z=float(np.sum(np.exp(s)))
   if z ==0: w = np.zeros(2*n-1); w[np.argmax(c)]=1; val=m
   else: w=np.exp(s)/z; val=m + np.log(z)/alpha
   return val,c,w
```
Since max s=0, z≥1 unless underflow? max exp(0)=1, so z≥1. Good. If alpha huge, s for submax maybe -inf, exp 0. `z` finite.
Could avoid computing c? Need c for weights. fine.

def _lse_obj(x,n,alpha,state):

   state['w']=w; state['x']=np.array(x,copy=True); state['n']=n
   return val
```

def _lse_grad(x,n,alpha,state):
   xold=state.get('x')
   if xold is None or xold.shape != x.shape or not np.array_equal(xold,x):
      val,c,w=lse_parts(x,n,alpha)
      state['w']=w; state['x']=np.array(x,copy=True)
   else:
      w=state['w']
   return grad_weighted(x,n,w)
```

def slsqp_one(h,n,alpha,maxiter):
   h=project_exact(h,n)
   state={}
   bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
   cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':_sum_eq,'jac':_sum_jac,'args':(n,)}]
   try:
      res=minimize(_lse_obj, h, jac=_lse_grad, args=(n,alpha,state), method='SLSQP', bounds=bounds, constraints=cons, options={'maxiter':maxiter, 'ftol':1e-12, 'disp':False})
      x=res.x
      if x is None: return h
      return project_exact(x,n)
   except Exception:
      return h
```
SLSQP may pass x as shape (n,1)? Usually 1D. `project_exact` flatten.

`project_exact`:
```
def project_exact(h,n):
    h=np.asarray(h,dtype=float).ravel().copy()
    target=0.5*n
    lo=float(np.min(h))-1.0
    hi=float(np.max(h))+1.0
    # if target near 0/n, but h in [0,1] target n/2. okay.
    for _ in range(50):
        mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
        s=np.clip(h-mid,0.0,1.0).sum()
        if s>target:
            lo=mid
        else:
            hi=mid
    y=np.clip(h-0.5*(lo+hi),0.0,1.0)
    res=target-y.sum()
    if res>0:
       mask=(y<1.0-1e-15)
       if np.any(mask):
           y[mask]+=res/np.count_nonzero(mask)
       else:
           y[np.argmax(y)]+=res
    elif res<0:
       mask=(y>1e-15)
       if np.any(mask):
           y[mask]+=res/np.count_nonzero(mask)
       else:
           y[np.argmin(y)]+=res
    y=np.clip(y,0.0,1.0)
    # final exact adjust one coordinate to fix floating
    res=target-y.sum()
    if res>0:
       idx=int(np.argmax(1.0-y)); y[idx]+=res
    elif res<0:
       idx=int(np.argmax(y)); y[idx]+=res
    return np.clip(y,0.0,1.0)
```
If adding res to argmax room overflow, clip after may make sum off. But res tiny. Could after clip if still off, adjust again:
```
res=target-y.sum(); if res>0: idx...; y[idx]+=res
```
We can loop 3.

But if y all at 1 and target<n? Not possible target n/2. If res small.

For SLSQP, clipping after projection may make h slightly infeasible if res >1-y[idx]? res tiny. okay.

Potential issue: Bisection with h outside [0,1] and target n/2, lo/hi as min-1 to max+1 works. If h large? from optimizer within bounds. Good.

`make_radial(n, kind, a=0.3)`:
```
def make_radial(n, kind, a=0.3):
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*2.0/n
    r=np.abs(x-1.0)
    if kind==0: h=1.0-r
    elif kind==1:
       if a<=0: h=np.maximum(0.0,1.0-r)
       elif a>=1: h=np.ones(n)
       else:
          h=np.where(r<=a,1.0,np.maximum(0.0,1.0-(r-a)/(1.0-a)))
    elif kind==2:
       f=r*r*(3.0-2.0*r); h=1.0-f
    elif kind==3:
       f=r*r*(3.0-2.0*r); h=f
    elif kind==4:
       h=0.5+0.5*np.cos(np.pi*r)  # average? ∫ cos πr not zero; project
    else: h=np.full(n,0.5); h=np.clip
    return project_exact(h,n)
```
Values maybe outside [0,1]? cos πr ∈[-1,1], h [0,1], average: 0.5+0.5∫cosπr dr=0.5+0? ∫0^1 cosπr=0, yes area1. Center r=0 h=1, ends0; like cosine radial. Include. Kind 4 no average projection needed. Kind 5 sine? h=0.5+0.5*sin? Could include random.

def resample(h, oldn, newn):
   xo=(np.arange(oldn)+0.5)*2.0/oldn
   xn=(np.arange(newn)+0.5)*2.0/newn
   return project_exact(np.interp(xn,xo,h),newn)
```

`spectral_random(n, M, rng, amp=1.0)` maybe include:
```
def spectral_random(n,M,rng,amp=0.8):
   y=np.full(n,0.5)
   t=np.arange(n)
   for m in range(1,M+1):
      a=rng.normal(0.0, amp/float(m+1))
      b=rng.normal(0.0, amp/float(m+1))
      th=2.0*np.pi*m*t/n
      y += a*np.cos(th)+b*np.sin(th)
   return project_exact(y,n)
```
No long lists.

`gradient_pair_refine` final. Need be careful with time. Implement robust with p and temp schedule:
```
def gradient_pair_refine(h,n,deadline,p=12,temp=0.004):
    h=project_exact(h.copy(),n)
    best=compute_c5(h,n)
    no_improve=0
    while time.time()<deadline:
       c=compute_c_vec(h,n)
       m=float(np.max(c))
       idx=np.argsort(c)[-2*p:][::-1]
       val=(c[idx]-m)/temp
       val-=val.max()
       ww=np.exp(val)/np.sum(np.exp(val))
       w=np.zeros(2*n-1)
       w[idx]=ww
       g=grad_weighted(h,n,w)
       g-=g.mean()
       src=np.argsort(g)[-p:][::-1]
       tgt=np.argsort(g)[:p]
       improved=False
       for s in src:
          hs=h[s]
          if hs<=1e-12: continue
          for t in tgt:
             if t==s: continue
             room=1.0-h[t]
             cap=min(hs,room)
             if cap<=1e-12: continue
             for frac in (0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01):
                d=cap*frac
                if d<=1e-14: continue
                h[s]-=d; h[t]+=d
                c2=compute_c5(h,n)
                if c2 < best - 1e-14:
                   best=c2; improved=True
                   h=project_exact(h,n)
                   break
                h[s]+=d; h[t]-=d
             if improved:
                break
          if improved:
             break  # restart pass
       if improved:
          no_improve=0
       else:
          no_improve+=1
          temp=max(0.0005,temp*0.6)
          if no_improve>4: break
    return h,best
```
This recomputes after each accept. If no improvement, reduce temp focusing near-max. If many no_improve, break. Time could be spent in loops evaluating many pairs before finding improvement. ### Verify gradient formula scaling with weights sum 1

The smooth objective Φ = m + log(z)/α. We are using weights w = exp(s)/z, where s=α(c-m). Gradient of Φ w.r.t h = Σ w ∇c_h? Let's verify derivative of log(z)/α = (1/z) Σ w α ∇c? Actually z=Σ e^{α c_j}. ∂(log z)/∂h = (1/α? wait d log z = Σ e^{αc} α ∂c /z = α Σ w ∂c. Then divided by α? In objective m + log(z)/α: derivative = ∂m? earlier cancellation gave Σ w ∂c. Let's re-derive: Φ = m + (1/α) log z. ∂Φ = ∂m + (1/α)*(1/z)Σ e^{αc} α ∂c_j = ∂m + Σ w ∂c_j - (Σ w ∂m)∂m? Since ∂m = one_hot at argmax, and Σ w=1, ∂m = Σ w ∂m? Actually ∂m is vector with 1 at max; sum w_j ∂m/∂c_j = 1. So ∂m - Σ w ∂m =0. Leaves Σ w ∂c_j. Good. Weights sum 1. `grad_weighted` returns dx*Σ weights * ∇(unscaled terms). Since c=term*dx, ∇c=dx∇term. Good.

def make_periodic(n, period, a=0.0):
   x=(np.arange(n)+.5)*2/n
   y=x % period

Add top-level `make_periodic(n,p)`.

```
def make_periodic(n,p):
   x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*2.0/n
   y=x % p
   r=np.abs(y-0.5*p)
   s=np.clip(2*r/p,0,1)
   f=s*s*(3-2*s)
   h=1-f
   return project_exact(h,n)
```

### Let's consider a potential **bug in project_exact bisection direction**

We want y=clip(h-λ,0,1). If λ low, h-λ high -> y=1 sum n. If λ high ->0. We set lo (low λ) sum high, hi high sum low. In loop: s>target -> sum too high -> need increase λ -> lo=mid. s<target -> hi=mid. Correct. If target=n/2, okay. After bisection, y close target.

### Compact project_exact

```
def project_exact(h,n):
    h=np.asarray(h,float).ravel()
    target=.5*n
    lo=min(float(h.min())-1,1.0); hi=max(float(h.max())+1,1.0)
    for _ in range(45):
        mid=.5*(lo+hi)
        s=np.clip(h-mid,0,1).sum()
        if s>target: lo=mid
        else: hi=mid
    y=np.clip(h-.5*(lo+hi),0,1)
    for _ in range(3):
        r=target-y.sum()
        if abs(r)<1e-13: break
        if r>0:
            m=y<1-1e-15; idx=np.argmax(1-y) if not m.any() else None
            if m.any(): y[m]+=r/m.sum()
            else: y[idx]+=r
        else:
            m=y>1e-15; idx=np.argmax(y) if not m.any() else None
            if m.any(): y[m]+=r/m.sum()
            else: y[idx]+=r
    return np.clip(y,0,1)
```
Potential if r big and distributing to many may push beyond bounds. Use `np.clip` after. Sum after clip maybe not exact; loop only 3. But bisection residual small. Good.
If `h` empty? no.

`lo=min(float(h.min())-1,1.0)`: If h min 0, lo=-1; okay. If h max 1, hi=2. Good.

Maybe bisection 45 enough.

```
def slsqp_one(h,n,alpha,mi):
    state={}; h=project_exact(h,n)
    try:
       res=minimize(_lse_obj,h,jac=_lse_grad,args=(n,alpha,state),method='SLSQP',bounds=[(0.,1.)]*n,constraints=[{'type':'eq','fun':_sum_eq,'jac':_sum_jac,'args':(n,)}],options={'maxiter':mi,'ftol':1e-12,'disp':False})
       return project_exact(res.x,n) if res.x is not None else h
    except Exception: return h
```
Line long but okay.

### Let's maybe implement a **quick full-dimensional Newton-like** using active weights and solving linear system?

At active weights w, the KKT for interior with sum: ∑w_k ∇c_k = λ 1 + bounds. If h interior and unconstrained except sum, we can solve for h? The weighted objective gradient = g = dx (w0+shifts...). Setting g constant across coordinates (after subtract mean). This is a nonlinear equation because g linear in h? Yes derivative of each term c_k is **linear** in h! The smooth objective gradient is linear in h: g_i = const - dx*(something h). Specifically for weights, g_i = dx( w0 + sum terms with 1 - ...). Actually c_k gradient is affine in h (constant plus - sums). Thus the smooth objective Φ is a quadratic function! Wait each c_k is quadratic, but its gradient is linear. With fixed weights w, the LSE approximation Φ=m+logsumexp(αc)/α is not quadratic because weights depend on h. But if we fix weights w, the surrogate S_w(h)=Σ w_k c_k is quadratic (with negative cross terms). We could minimize S_w over [0,1] sum. This is a quadratic program (not convex maybe, but can solve via gradient linear system if interior). Since gradient affine, stationary interior solution is linear system: g(h)=constant. If cross terms make matrix indefinite, might be local saddle. But we can iterate: compute active w, solve/gradient step to minimize S_w exactly? This could be faster and global within weights.

Can we solve min S_w(h) easily? S_w = const - quadratic form? Let's derive: c_k terms. Gradient g(h)=B - A h. Stationary ∂(S_w)/∂h under sum constraint: B - A h = λ 1. If A has special Toeplitz structure. Inverting A maybe possible via FFT. But bounds complicate. We can use this as a line search direction: Given w, direction that reduces S_w is -(g - mean). Since S_w quadratic and concave/convex? If -A is indefinite, steepest may not be good. But we already use gradient of LSE (weights change).

Maybe implement **fixed-weight quadratic minimization via few Newton steps**:
- Given w, direction d = -(g - mean). Use line search with S_w (not full LSE) to move, then recompute weights. This is like Frank-Wolfe for max. Could be efficient. But SLSQP essentially does Newton on LSE.

```
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time

def compute_c5(h,n):
    dx=2.0/n
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full'))*dx)

def compute_c_vec(h,n):
    dx=2.0/n
    A=np.correlate(h,h,mode='full')
    pref=np.empty(n+1); pref[0]=0.0
    if n>0: pref[1:]=np.cumsum(h)
    total=pref[n]
    c=np.empty(2*n-1)
    c[n-1]=(total-A[n-1])*dx
    if n>1:
        ks=np.arange(1,n)
        Apos=A[n:]
        p=pref[n-ks]
        pk=(p-Apos)*dx
        nk=(total-p-Apos)*dx
        c[n:]=pk
        c[:n-1]=nk[::-1]
    return c
```
For n=1, c length1, A[n:] A[1:] empty, but code not executed. `c[:0]` fine. `pref[n]` ok.

`project_exact` compact:
```
def project_exact(h,n):
    h=np.asarray(h,dtype=float).ravel()
    target=0.5*float(n)
    lo=float(h.min())-1.0
    hi=float(h.max())+1.0
    for _ in range(45):
        mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
        s=np.clip(h-mid,0.0,1.0).sum()
        if s>target:
            lo=mid
        else:
            hi=mid
    y=np.clip(h-0.5*(lo+hi),0.0,1.0)
    for _ in range(3):
        r=target-y.sum()
        if abs(r)<1e-13: break
        if r>0:
            m=y<1.0-1e-15
            if m.any(): y[m]+=r/float(m.sum())
            else: y[int(np.argmax(1.0-y))]+=r
        else:
            m=y>1e-15
            if m.any(): y[m]+=r/float(m.sum())
            else: y[int(np.argmax(y))]+=r
    return np.clip(y,0.0,1.0)
```
If h length n=0? h.min error. Not.

`grad_weighted`:
```
def grad_weighted(h,n,w):
    g=np.zeros(n)
    w0=w[n-1]
    if w0!=0.0:
        g+=w0*(1.0-2.0*h)
    for k in range(1,n):
        wp=w[n-1+k]
        wn=w[n-1-k]
        ws=wp+wn
        if ws==0.0: continue
        nv=n-k
        g[:nv]+=wp-ws*h[k:]
        g[k:]+=wn-ws*h[:nv]
    return (2.0/float(n))*g
```

`lse_parts`:
```
def lse_parts(x,n,alpha):
    c=compute_c_vec(x,n)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    s=alpha*(c-m)
    z=float(np.sum(np.exp(s)))
    if z<=0.0:
       w=np.zeros(2*n-1); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0; return m,c,w
    w=np.exp(s)/z
    val=m+np.log(z)/float(alpha)
    return float(val),c,w
```
Since max s=0, z>=1. But if c contains nan? no. If alpha=0? Not.

`_lse_obj/grad` as above.

`slsqp_one` uses `bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n`, `constraints` with dict. For n large, list length. Good.

`make_radial`:
```
def make_radial(n,kind=0,a=0.3):
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*2.0/n
    r=np.abs(x-1.0)
    if kind==0: h=1.0-r
    elif kind==1:
       if a<=0.0: h=np.maximum(0.0,1.0-r)
       elif a>=1.0: h=np.ones(n)
       else:
          h=np.where(r<=a,1.0,np.maximum(0.0,1.0-(r-a)/(1.0-a)))
    elif kind==2:
       f=r*r*(3.0-2.0*r); h=1.0-f
    elif kind==3:
       f=r*r*(3.0-2.0*r); h=f
    elif kind==4:
       h=0.5+0.5*np.cos(np.pi*r)
    else:
       h=np.full(n,0.5)
    return project_exact(h,n)
```
For kind1 with a=0.5, h=1 at center, ramp to 0 at ends. Good. `r` max ≤1? Midpoints max <1? For n, x max=2-dx, r=1-dx/2 <1, so h>0 at ends maybe not zero; area projection adjusts. If we want endpoints exactly zero, use linearly interpolated x endpoints? Midpoints okay.

`make_periodic`:
```
def make_periodic(n,p):
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*2.0/n
    y=x % p
    r=np.abs(y-0.5*p)
    s=np.clip(2.0*r/p,0.0,1.0)
    f=s*s*(3.0-2.0*s)
    return project_exact(1.0-f,n)
```
If p>2? no.

`spectral_random`:
```
def spectral_random(n,m,rng,amp=0.8):
    y=np.full(n,0.5)
    t=np.arange(n)
    for q in range(1,m+1):
       a=rng.normal(0.0,amp/float(q+1)); b=rng.normal(0.0,amp/float(q+1))
       th=2.0*np.pi*float(q)*t/float(n)
       y+=a*np.cos(th)+b*np.sin(th)
    return project_exact(y,n)
```

`resample`:
```
def resample(h,oldn,newn):
    xo=(np.arange(oldn)+0.5)*2.0/oldn
    xn=(np.arange(newn)+0.5)*2.0/newn
    return project_exact(np.interp(xn,xo,h),newn)
```

`make_sym`:
```
def make_sym(h,n): return project_exact(0.5*(h+h[::-1]),n)
```

`add_candidate`:
```
def add_candidate(cands,h,n):
    h=project_exact(h,n)
    c=compute_c5(h,n)
    cands.append([c,len(cands),h,n])
```

`local_refine`:
```
def local_refine(h,n,deadline,quick=False):
    h=project_exact(h.copy(),n)
    best=compute_c5(h,n)
    if quick:
       steps=[(20,50),(100,80),(500,100),(2000,120)]
    else:
       steps=[(20,60),(100,100),(500,130),(2000,180),(10000,220),(50000,260),(100000,300)]
    scale=300.0/max(float(n),1.0)
    stall=0
    for alpha,mi in steps:
        if time.time()>=deadline: break
        mi=min(mi, max(20, int(mi*scale)))
        if mi<20: mi=20
        h2=slsqp_one(h,n,alpha,mi)
        c2=compute_c5(h2,n)
        if c2<best-1e-15:
            h=h2; best=c2; stall=0
        else:
            stall+=1
            if stall>=4 and alpha>500: break
    if time.time()<deadline-1:
       pe=min(time.time()+ (12 if quick else 50), deadline)
       p=8 if quick else 12
       h,best=gradient_pair_refine(h,n,pe,p,0.003 if quick else 0.002)
    h=project_exact(h,n); best=compute_c5(h,n)
    return h,best
```
Note `pe=min(time.time()+..., deadline)` if time.time()+... > deadline, okay. If pe <= time.time(), gradient_pair may not run? It will check time.

`gradient_pair_refine(h,n,deadline,p=12,temp=0.003)`:
Need define before local_refine? Python functions can call later-defined top-level if defined before run? At call time, yes all defined. But local_refine calls gradient_pair_refine defined later; okay as long before run executes. To be safe define gradient before local. We'll order.

`gradient_pair_refine` code:
```
def gradient_pair_refine(h,n,deadline,p=12,temp=0.003):
    h=project_exact(h.copy(),n)
    best=compute_c5(h,n)
    noimp=0
    cnt=min(2*p,2*n-1)
    fracs=(0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01)
    while time.time()<deadline:
        c=compute_c_vec(h,n)
        m=float(np.max(c))
        idx=np.argsort(c)[-cnt:]
        vv=(c[idx]-m)/temp
        vv-=vv.max()
        ee=np.exp(vv); ww=ee/float(ee.sum())
        w=np.zeros(2*n-1); w[idx]=ww
        g=grad_weighted(h,n,w)
        g-=np.mean(g)
        src=np.argsort(g)[-p:]
        tgt=np.argsort(g)[:p]
        improved=False
        for si in src:
            if improved: break
            hs=h[si]
            if hs<=1e-12: continue
            for tj in tgt:
                if si==tj: continue
                cap=hs if hs<1.0-h[tj] else 1.0-h[tj]
                if cap<=1e-12: continue
                for fr in fracs:
                    d=cap*fr
                    if d<=1e-14: continue
                    h[si]-=d; h[tj]+=d
                    c2=compute_c5(h,n)
                    if c2<best-1e-14:
                        best=c2; improved=True
                        break
                    h[si]+=d; h[tj]-=d
                if improved: break
        if improved:
            noimp=0
        else:
            noimp+=1
            temp=max(0.0004,temp*0.6)
            if noimp>4: break
    return project_exact(h,n), compute_c5(h,n)
```
Potential issue: After an accepted move, we don't project inside; but h still sum exact and bounds. `hs` for next source target stale but if move within same source? We break all loops after improved, so restart. Good.
`src=np.argsort(g)[-p:]` sorted ascending; last p are largest. `tgt=np.argsort(g)[:p]` smallest. Good.
If p > n? p small.
If noimp break, temp local variable changes but function returns; okay.

### Final `run`

Need no nested functions. Draft:
```
def run(seed=42,budget_s=1100,**kwargs):
    t0=time.time(); deadline=t0+float(budget_s)-3.0
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    try:
        n0=int(len(initial_h_values))
        h0=np.array(initial_h_values,dtype=float).ravel()
        if n0<10: raise ValueError
    except Exception:
        n0=300; h0=make_radial(300,2)
    if n0<100:
        h0=resample(h0,n0,300); n0=300
    elif n0>512:
        h0=resample(h0,n0,384); n0=384
    best_h=project_exact(h0,n0)
    best_n=n0
    best_c=compute_c5(best_h,best_n)
    cands=[]
    add_candidate(cands,best_h,n0)
    add_candidate(cands,make_sym(best_h,n0),n0)
    # radial search
    for a in np.arange(0.0,1.01,0.1):
        add_candidate(cands,make_radial(n0,1,float(a)),n0)
    add_candidate(cands,make_radial(n0,0),n0)
    add_candidate(cands,make_radial(n0,2),n0)
    add_candidate(cands,make_radial(n0,3),n0)
    add_candidate(cands,make_radial(n0,4),n0)
    # periodic
    for pp in (1.0,0.5,2.0/3.0):
        add_candidate(cands,make_periodic(n0,pp),n0)
    # random spectral
    for _ in range(3):
        add_candidate(cands,spectral_random(n0,7,rng,0.9),n0)
    # resampled initial
    if n0>256:
        for nn in (256,384):
            add_candidate(cands,resample(best_h,n0,nn),nn)
    else:
        for nn in (384,):
            add_candidate(cands,resample(best_h,n0,nn),nn)
    cands.sort()
    base_c=min(best_c,cands[0][0])
    processed=0
    for cand in cands:
        rem=deadline-time.time()
        if rem<100 or processed>=4: break
        if cand[0] > base_c + 0.02: break
        hh=project_exact(cand[2],cand[3]); nn=cand[3]
        same=(nn==best_n and np.array_equal(hh,best_h))
        if same: continue
        end_min=time.time()+55.0
        end=min(end_min, deadline-300.0)
        if end<=time.time(): continue
        hh,c2=local_refine(hh,nn,end,quick=True)
        if c2<best_c-1e-15:
            best_c=c2; best_h=hh; best_n=nn; base_c=min(base_c,c2)
        processed+=1
    rem=deadline-time.time()
    if rem>250:
        end=min(time.time()+380.0, deadline-150.0)
        best_h,best_c=local_refine(best_h,best_n,end,quick=False)
    rem=deadline-time.time()
    if rem>120:
        end=min(time.time()+150.0, deadline-40.0)
        best_h,best_c=gradient_pair_refine(best_h,best_n,end,14,0.0015)
    rem=deadline-time.time()
    if rem>100:
        end=min(time.time()+120.0, deadline-20.0)
        best_h,best_c=local_refine(best_h,best_n,end,quick=False)
    rem=deadline-time.time()
    if rem>25:
        end=min(time.time()+30.0, deadline-1.0)
        best_h,best_c=gradient_pair_refine(best_h,best_n,end,20,0.0008)
    best_h=project_exact(best_h,best_n)
    best_c=compute_c5(best_h,best_n)
    return best_h,best_c,best_n
```

```python
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time

def compute_c5(h, n):
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')) * dx)

def compute_c_vec(h, n):
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    A = np.correlate(h, h, mode='full')
    pref = np.empty(n + 1)
    pref[0] = 0.0
    if n:
        pref[1:] = np.cumsum(h)
    total = pref[n]
    c = np.empty(2 * n - 1)
    c[n - 1] = (total - A[n - 1]) * dx
    if n > 1:
        ks = np.arange(1, n)
        Apos = A[n:]
        p = pref[n - ks]
        pk = (p - Apos) * dx
        nk = (total - p - Apos) * dx
        c[n:] = pk
        c[:n - 1] = nk[::-1]
    return c

def project_exact(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).ravel()
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    lo = float(h.min()) - 1.0
    hi = float(h.max()) + 1.0
    for _ in range(45):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        s = np.clip(h - mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum()
        if s > target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    y = np.clip(h - 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    for _ in range(3):
        r = target - y.sum()
        if abs(r) < 1e-13:
            break
        if r > 0:
            m = y < 1.0 - 1e-15
            if m.any():
                y[m] += r / float(m.sum())
            else:
                y[int(np.argmax(1.0 - y))] += r
        else:
            m = y > 1e-15
            if m.any():
                y[m] += r / float(m.sum())
            else:
                y[int(np.argmax(y))] += r
    return np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0)

def grad_weighted(h, n, w):
    g = np.zeros(n)
    w0 = w[n - 1]
    if w0 != 0.0:
        g += w0 * (1.0 - 2.0 * h)
    for k in range(1, n):
        wp = w[n - 1 + k]
        wn = w[n - 1 - k]
        ws = wp + wn
        if ws == 0.0:
            continue
        nv = n - k
        g[:nv] += wp - ws * h[k:]
        g[k:] += wn - ws * h[:nv]
    return (2.0 / float(n)) * g

def lse_parts(x, n, alpha):
    c = compute_c_vec(x, n)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        w = np.zeros(2 * n - 1)
        w[0] = 1.0
        return 1e100, c, w
    s = alpha * (c - m)
    z = float(np.sum(np.exp(s)))
    if z <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(2 * n - 1)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return m, c, w
    w = np.exp(s) / z
    val = m + np.log(z) / float(alpha)
    return float(val), c, w

def _lse_obj(x, n, alpha, state):
    val, c, w = lse_parts(x, n, alpha)
    state['w'] = w
    state['x'] = np.array(x, dtype=float, copy=True)
    return val

def _lse_grad(x, n, alpha, state):
    xold = state.get('x')
    if xold is None or xold.shape != x.shape or not np.array_equal(xold, x):
        val, c, w = lse_parts(x, n, alpha)
        state['w'] = w
        state['x'] = np.array(x, dtype=float, copy=True)
    else:
        w = state['w']
    return grad_weighted(x, n, w)

def _sum_eq(x, n):
    return float(np.sum(x) - 0.5 * float(n))

def _sum_jac(x, n):
    return np.ones(n)

def slsqp_one(h, n, alpha, maxiter):
    h = project_exact(h, n)
    state = {}
    try:
        res = minimize(
            _lse_obj, h, jac=_lse_grad, args=(n, alpha, state),
            method='SLSQP', bounds=[(0.0, 1.0)] * n,
            constraints=[{'type': 'eq', 'fun': _sum_eq, 'jac': _sum_jac, 'args': (n,)}],
            options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'ftol': 1e-12, 'disp': False})
        if res.x is None:
            return h
        return project_exact(res.x, n)
    except Exception:
        return h

def gradient_pair_refine(h, n, deadline, p=12, temp=0.003):
    h = project_exact(h.copy(), n)
    best = compute_c5(h, n)
    noimp = 0
    cnt = min(2 * p, 2 * n - 1)
    fracs = (0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.05, 0.02, 0.01)
    while time.time() < deadline:
        c = compute_c_vec(h, n)
        m = float(np.max(c))
        idx = np.argsort(c)[-cnt:]
        vv = (c[idx] - m) / temp
        vv -= vv.max()
        ee = np.exp(vv)
        ww = ee / float(ee.sum())
        w = np.zeros(2 * n - 1)
        w[idx] = ww
        g = grad_weighted(h, n, w)
        g -= float(np.mean(g))
        src = np.argsort(g)[-p:]
        tgt = np.argsort(g)[:p]
        improved = False
        for si in src:
            if improved:
                break
            hs = h[si]
            if hs <= 1e-12:
                continue
            for tj in tgt:
                if si == tj:
                    continue
                cap = hs if hs < 1.0 - h[tj] else 1.0 - h[tj]
                if cap <= 1e-12:
                    continue
                for fr in fracs:
                    d = cap * fr
                    if d <= 1e-14:
                        continue
                    h[si] -= d
                    h[tj] += d
                    c2 = compute_c5(h, n)
                    if c2 < best - 1e-14:
                        best = c2
                        improved = True
                        break
                    h[si] += d
                    h[tj] -= d
                if improved:
                    break
        if improved:
            noimp = 0
        else:
            noimp += 1
            temp = max(0.0004, temp * 0.6)
            if noimp > 4:
                break
    hp = project_exact(h, n)
    cp = compute_c5(hp, n)
    if cp < best:
        h = hp
        best = cp
    return h, best

def local_refine(h, n, deadline, quick=False):
    h = project_exact(h.copy(), n)
    best = compute_c5(h, n)
    if quick:
        steps = ((20, 50), (100, 80), (500, 100), (2000, 120))
    else:
        steps = ((20, 60), (100, 100), (500, 130), (2000, 180),
                 (10000, 220), (50000, 260), (100000, 300))
    scale = 300.0 / max(float(n), 1.0)
    stall = 0
    for alpha, mi in steps:
        if time.time() >= deadline:
            break
        mi = min(mi, max(20, int(float(mi) * scale)))
        h2 = slsqp_one(h, n, alpha, mi)
        c2 = compute_c5(h2, n)
        if c2 < best - 1e-15:
            h = h2
            best = c2
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall >= 4 and alpha > 500:
                break
    if time.time() < deadline - 1:
        pe = min(time.time() + (12.0 if quick else 50.0), deadline)
        pp = 8 if quick else 12
        tt = 0.003 if quick else 0.002
        h, best = gradient_pair_refine(h, n, pe, pp, tt)
    hp = project_exact(h, n)
    cp = compute_c5(hp, n)
    if cp < best:
        h = hp
        best = cp
    return h, best

def make_radial(n, kind=0, a=0.3):
    x = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) * 2.0 / float(n)
    r = np.abs(x - 1.0)
    if kind == 0:
        h = 1.0 - r
    elif kind == 1:
        if a <= 0.0:
            h = np.maximum(0.0, 1.0 - r)
        elif a >= 1.0:
            h = np.ones(n)
        else:
            h = np.where(r <= a, 1.0, np.maximum(0.0, 1.0 - (r - a) / (1.0 - a)))
    elif kind == 2:
        f = r * r * (3.0 - 2.0 * r)
        h = 1.0 - f
    elif kind == 3:
        f = r * r * (3.0 - 2.0 * r)
        h = f
    elif kind == 4:
        h = 0.5 + 0.5 * np.cos(np.pi * r)
    else:
        h = np.full(n, 0.5)
    return project_exact(h, n)

def make_periodic(n, p):
    x = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) * 2.0 / float(n)
    y = x % p
    r = np.abs(y - 0.5 * p)
    s = np.clip(2.0 * r / p, 0.0, 1.0)
    f = s * s * (3.0 - 2.0 * s)
    return project_exact(1.0 - f, n)

def spectral_random(n, m, rng, amp=0.8):
    y = np.full(n, 0.5)
    t = np.arange(n)
    for q in range(1, m + 1):
        a = rng.normal(0.0, amp / float(q + 1))
        b = rng.normal(0.0, amp / float(q + 1))
        th = 2.0 * np.pi * float(q) * t / float(n)
        y += a * np.cos(th) + b * np.sin(th)
    return project_exact(y, n)

def resample(h, oldn, newn):
    xo = (np.arange(oldn) + 0.5) * 2.0 / float(oldn)
    xn = (np.arange(newn) + 0.5) * 2.0 / float(newn)
    return project_exact(np.interp(xn, xo, h), newn)

def make_sym(h, n):
    return project_exact(0.5 * (h + h[::-1]), n)

def add_candidate(cands, h, n):
    h = project_exact(h, n)
    c = compute_c5(h, n)
    cands.append([c, len(cands), h, n])

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    deadline = t0 + float(budget_s) - 3.0
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    try:
        n0 = int(len(initial_h_values))
        h0 = np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float).ravel()
        if n0 < 10:
            raise ValueError
    except Exception:
        n0 = 300
        h0 = make_radial(300, 2)
    if n0 < 100:
        h0 = resample(h0, n0, 300)
        n0 = 300
    elif n0 > 512:
        h0 = resample(h0, n0, 384)
        n0 = 384

    best_h = project_exact(h0, n0)
    best_n = n0
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, best_n)

    cands = []
    add_candidate(cands, best_h, n0)
    add_candidate(cands, make_sym(best_h, n0), n0)
    for a in np.arange(0.0, 1.01, 0.1):
        add_candidate(cands, make_radial(n0, 1, float(a)), n0)
    add_candidate(cands, make_radial(n0, 0), n0)
    add_candidate(cands, make_radial(n0, 2), n0)
    add_candidate(cands, make_radial(n0, 3), n0)
    add_candidate(cands, make_radial(n0, 4), n0)
    for pp in (1.0, 0.5, 2.0 / 3.0):
        add_candidate(cands, make_periodic(n0, pp), n0)
    for _ in range(3):
        add_candidate(cands, spectral_random(n0, 7, rng, 0.9), n0)
    if n0 > 256:
        for nn in (256, 384):
            add_candidate(cands, resample(best_h, n0, nn), nn)
    else:
        add_candidate(cands, resample(best_h, n0, 384), 384)

    cands.sort()
    if len(cands) and cands[0][0] < best_c:
        best_c = cands[0][0]
        best_h = project_exact(cands[0][2], cands[0][3])
        best_n = cands[0][3]
    base_c = min(best_c, cands[0][0] if cands else best_c)

    processed = 0
    for cand in cands:
        rem = deadline - time.time()
        if rem < 100 or processed >= 4:
            break
        if cand[0] > base_c + 0.02:
            break
        hh = project_exact(cand[2], cand[3])
        nn = cand[3]
        if nn == best_n and np.array_equal(hh, best_h):
            continue
        end = min(time.time() + 55.0, deadline - 300.0)
        if end <= time.time():
            continue
        hh, c2 = local_refine(hh, nn, end, quick=True)
        if c2 < best_c - 1e-15:
            best_c = c2
            best_h = hh
            best_n = nn
            base_c = min(base_c, c2)
        processed += 1

    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if rem > 250:
        end = min(time.time() + 350.0, deadline - 170.0)
        best_h, best_c = local_refine(best_h, best_n, end, quick=False)

    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if rem > 220 and best_n < 512:
        nn = min(512, best_n + 128)
        end = min(time.time() + 110.0, deadline - 150.0)
        hh = resample(best_h, best_n, nn)
        hh, c2 = local_refine(hh, nn, end, quick=True)
        if c2 < best_c - 1e-15:
            best_c = c2
            best_h = hh
            best_n = nn

    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if rem > 120:
        end = min(time.time() + 130.0, deadline - 50.0)
        best_h, best_c = gradient_pair_refine(best_h, best_n, end, 14, 0.0015)

    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if rem > 90:
        end = min(time.time() + 85.0, deadline - 25.0)
        best_h, best_c = local_refine(best_h, best_n, end, quick=False)

    rem = deadline - time.time()
    if rem > 25:
        end = min(time.time() + 25.0, deadline - 1.0)
        best_h, best_c = gradient_pair_refine(best_h, best_n, end, 20, 0.0008)

    best_h = project_exact(best_h, best_n)
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, best_n)
    return best_h, best_c, best_n
```
